MSCI 降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待
2026 年 10 月 27 日
一、 市場動態
1. MSCI 擬將印尼降至獨立市場，降評風險明顯升高：MSCI 於 10 月 15 日宣布，啟動
將印尼股市由「新興市場（Emerging Market）」直接降至「獨立市場（Standalone
Market） 」的諮詢程序，意見徵詢將於2027 年 4 月 30 日截止，最晚於 2027 年 6 月
30 日公布最終決定 。MSCI 原先於今年 1 月提出可能降至 「邊境市場 （Frontier Market
） 」 ，此次則跳過「邊境市場」提出直接將該國股市降至「獨立市場」的方案，顯示對

印尼市場治理的疑慮進一步加深。雖然「邊境市場」仍可能是替代分類方案，但MSCI
強調，除非有明確證據顯示市場治理及運作出現實質改善，否則預期將依目前提案重
新分類，顯示此次降評風險較過去顯著升高。
2. 股權透明度與交易秩序問題為 MSCI 主要疑慮：MSCI 指出，印尼股市長期存在股權
結構不透明、實際自由流通股比例難以確認、疑似協調交易，以及市場監理執法成效
不足等問題，影響國際投資人對市場公平性與可投資性的信心。雖然印尼主管機關已
提出強化持股超過 1%股東資訊揭露、建立高持股集中度（HSC）監理機制，以及規
劃逐步將最低自由流通股比例提高至 15%等改革措施，但MSCI 認為相關改革尚未展
現持續且具實質性的改善成果。由於問題涉及市場制度與治理結構，改善所需時間可
能較一般短期市場波動更長。
3. 股市反彈後再度回落，外資撤離壓力仍高：受降評消息衝擊，印尼雅加達綜合指數 10
月 16 日重挫 11.57%，創 1998 年以來最大單日跌幅，19 日再跌 3.64%，盤中最低
觸及 4,907.37 點 。 雖然20 日至 23 日連續四個交易日反彈 ， 但26 日再度下跌 1.22%

，收於 5,373.09 點，各產業類股全面收黑，顯示市場信心仍不穩定。根據 Reuters
報導，截至 10 月 19 日，2026 年外資已累計淨賣超印尼股票約 48.8 億美元。此外，
印尼證交所 26 日宣布，將引進獨立專家及外國投資人代表參與高持股集中度審查，
並自 11 月起加強調查疑似協調交易，必要時凍結可疑帳戶，以回應 MSCI 對市場制
度的疑慮。
二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 股市：潛在指數剔除與資金外流，持續壓抑市場評價
若印尼股市最終降至「獨立市場」， 原則上將不再納入MSCI 新興市場及邊境市場相關
指數，追蹤相關指數的被動式基金將須依規則調整持股，實際處理方式仍以 MSCI 後
續公告為準。若改降至「邊境市場」，則可能獲得部分邊境市場資金承接，衝擊相對較
小。Reuters 引述多家金融機構估計，印尼股市降至獨立市場可能引發約 30 億至 60

不另行通知。
億美元被動資金流出，其中 UBS 估計約 40 億美元，並指出主動型基金另有約 150 億
美元潛在撤資風險，惟實際規模仍視最終分類結果及基金策略而定。
2. 匯率：外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力
印尼股市的降評風險不僅衝擊股市，也可能透過外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力。印尼
盾 10 月 16 日盤中一度貶至 18,070 兌 1 美元，為 7 月下旬以來最弱水準之一，當日
收盤約 18,000。若後續國際資金持續流出，可能進一步增加股匯市波動，對以外幣計
價、且未完全避險的印尼基金及 ETF 形成額外壓力。雖然印尼央行可透過匯市干預及

流動性管理措施穩定金融市場，但在資金外流壓力尚未緩解前，印尼盾仍可能維持偏
弱格局。
3. 基金與 ETF：指數權重下滑，單一市場曝險風險升高
MSCI 相關指數調整已對印尼股票產生實質影響。Reuters 引用 LSEG 資料顯示，印尼
在 MSCI 新興市場指數的權重已由 2026 年初的 1.16%降至 9 月底的 0.43%，相關成
分股亦由年初 18 檔減至 9 檔，反映部分指數資金調整壓力已提前出現。對印尼單一
國家基金及 ETF 而言，主要風險來自股市下跌、資金流出及印尼盾波動；主動式基金
雖可透過選股降低部分影響，但仍難以完全迴避整體市場風險。不同產品實際受到的
影響，仍應依追蹤指數、主要持股及匯率避險安排個別評估。
三、 市場看法：
1. 制度性問題難以速解，降評風險將持續牽動市場：我們認為，此次 MSCI 降評風險並
非單純反映經濟成長放緩或企業獲利不佳，而是涉及市場透明度、交易公平性與監理
執法等結構性問題。尤其 MSCI 不僅啟動降評諮詢，更持續限制印尼股票的部分指數

調整，包括不提高外資納入因子、不新增相關成分股，以及不進行向上規模分類調整
，顯示市場可投資性疑慮尚未獲得有效解決。儘管政府試圖推動改革，但制度修正仍
須經過執行與成效驗證，才能重新取得國際投資人的信任，因此短期內降評風險仍難
完全消除。
2. 外資撤離與指數權重下降，市場信心修復恐需較長時間：印尼今年以來外資已大幅賣
超，MSCI 新興市場指數中的權重亦明顯下降，顯示國際投資人對當地市場的疑慮已
逐漸反映於實際資金流向。我們認為，即使印尼最終成功避免降評，部分國際機構投
資人仍可能因市場透明度、流動性及治理品質疑慮而維持較低曝險。此外，MSCI 對
部分指數調整的凍結措施尚未解除，短期內新增指數資金流入機會仍受到限制，預期
國際資金回流與市場評價修復均可能需要較長時間。
3. 監理改革力度加大，但尚不足以扭轉保守展望：印尼政府近期持續加強改革，包括提
高資訊透明度、強化交易監控、對違規交易開罰，以及引進外部獨立人士參與監理審
查。10 月 26 日該國政府進一步提出主動調查疑似協調交易及凍結可疑帳戶等措施，

11 月 2 日起亦規劃將高持股集中度股票排除於雅加達綜合指數之外 。 相關改革有助改

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
善交易秩序及短期市場信心，但部分措施仍待正式實施，且實際成效尚未獲得 MSCI
認可。考量制度性問題改善需要時間，政策利多雖可能帶動短線反彈，但尚不足以改
變對印尼股市中期前景的保守判斷。
4. 股市跌深不代表利空出盡，中期風險報酬吸引力偏低：印尼股市在 10 月 16 日及 19
日大幅修正後，雖曾連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度回落，顯示市場尚未形成穩
定回升趨勢。我們認為，當前股市面臨的不僅是短期賣壓，更包括國際投資人信任下
降及風險溢價提高，即使企業基本面未同步惡化，仍可能因評價下修而承受股價壓力

。因此，短線不排除政策利多或跌深後出現技術性反彈，但在 MSCI 態度、外資流向
與印尼盾走勢明顯改善前，反彈延續性仍有疑慮，印尼股市中期展望維持保守。
四、市場展望：
1. 印尼股市維持保守展望，單一市場風險報酬吸引力不足：考量 MSCI 降評風險尚未解除、
外資撤離壓力持續 ， 以及市場治理改革仍有待驗證 ， 我們認為印尼股市未來數季的風險報
酬吸引力相對有限。即使近期股價明顯回落，仍難認定相關風險已充分反映。相較市場制
度成熟、國際資金流動性較佳的新興市場，印尼股市可能持續面臨較高風險溢價，因此對
單一印尼市場的集中曝險應維持審慎態度。
2. 此事件對新興市場指數與東協指數影響相對有限：根據 MSCI 資料，截至2026 年 9 月底
，印尼股市在 MSCI Emerging Market 指數與 MSCI AC ASEAN 指數中的權重分別約為
0.43%與 7.55%，因此我們認為印尼潛在降評對相關指數的直接衝擊相對有限。此外，新
興市場各國經濟結構與產業組成有所差異 ， 區域型基金可透過跨國分散降低單一市場風險

。惟後續仍須留意印尼事件對整體新興市場投資信心造成的外溢影響 ， 以及全球貿易與匯
率波動風險。

ab 12 October 2026
Global Research
Genius Electronic Optical
Core resilience with CPO upside optionality
Raise PT to NT$1,250 on potential CPO contribution and intact core business
We reiterate Buy and raise our  price target to NT$1,250 from NT$700, rolling valuation
to 2028E and moving from P/B to a P/E-based SOTP that separately values the core lens
business and the emerging co-packaged optics (CPO) business. We believe Genius is
making progress sampling three precision components inside the fiber array unit (FAU)

targeting FAU integrators such as Coherent and FOCI.  We see potential for the company
to qualify the leading FAU module supplier into NVIDIA's CPO program. At the same
time, its core business is outperforming Largan, growing +1% YoY in Q326 vs. -11%
YoY for Largan as its share rises on periscope lens. Compared with the pressure on
Largan's margins from green electricity prices, we believe Genius should maintain its
margin, as its main production site is in Xiamen, whereas Largan's production in Taiwan

is affected by higher electricity prices.
Core intact: periscope share gains offset muted smartphone demand
September sales of NT$2.67bn (−2% MoM, +1% YoY) took Q326 to NT$7.82bn (+38%
QoQ, +1% YoY), in-line with VA consensus. Genius grew 10% YoY in 9M26 against 2%
at Largan, which we attribute to share gains in periscope lenses for premium iPhones,
offsetting legacy price cuts and lower VR lens shipments. We still keep 7% below VA on
4Q26E revenue (NT$7.34bn, −6% QoQ) reflecting muted iPhone sales and no content

in the iPhone 18 Pro's new variable-aperture lens.
CPO component strategy offers a different route to market from Largan
Unlike Largan's pursuit of complete FAU assembly, Genius is targeting high precision
optical components within the module, including: 1) high-precision prism to collimate
light from the PIC into the fibers, 2) V-groove/key slot to precisely hold the fibers in place,
and 3) MT ferrules (high density plastic connector bundling and aligning fibers). We

believe Genius' customer is one of the key module suppliers on NVIDIA's 2H27 program
targeting over 50% share. Our base case models a complete-FAU market of US$1.85bn
in 2028E and US$4.45bn in 2030E, with Genius base case for US$185m and US$668m
representing 10% and 15% revenue share of the complete-FAU revenue TAM.
Valuation: Maintain Buy with  PT lifted to NT$1,250
We fine-tune our 2026/2027 basic EPS estimates from NT$37.2/NT$41.5 to NT$36.7/NT

$44.0 and introduce 2028E EPS of NT$49.25. We move from P/B to P/E SOTP as the core
business now delivers steadier earnings and CPO warrants separate valuation. Our NT
$1,250 PT comprises NT$745 base business  (15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) and NT$505
for CPO (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in 2028E). Catalysts include qualifications at
leading FAU makers and the board approved 7mn share private private placement with
potential strategic investor (albeit 6% dilution if issued). Our US$1.8bn CPO value

compares to US$7bn implied in our Largan NT$5,000 PT.

Genius Electronic Optical UBS Research
Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Could Genius' CPO products contribute meaningful revenue in a few years?
Yes, from H227E into 2028E. We see Genius' CPO strategy as feasible and lower-risk than Largan's.
Largan's goal is to supply the complete FAU; Genius is entering at the component level, supplying V-
grooves, MT ferrules and prisms to FAU makers. This lets Genius sell into multiple FAU designs and

integrators, limiting its downside if the market converges on a single winning architecture.
Management believes multi-sourcing is inevitable once volumes scale and has seen early indications
of potentially substantial demand from US optical players, including Corning and Coherent. Genius
has a partnership with FOCI and is pursuing other FAU integrators including we believe Coherent;
management suggested further updates could come in the near term. Some parts already ship in

small volumes, but we do not expect meaningful revenue until H227 or 2028.
Q: Will Genius benefit from further lens technology upgrades in the coming years?
Yes. We expect premium lens participation and product mix to support core revenue growth of 8.0%,
9.7% and 9.1% in 2026-28E. We believe Genius is gaining share from Largan in Apple's periscope
lens supply chain after initially lagging. Apple (70–80% of Genius' sales) sell-through units grew 9%

YoY YTD through August according to Counterpoint, and Genius' 9M26 revenue grew 10% YoY on
a similar trajectory. Largan grew only 2% in 9M26, with Q326 down 11% YoY. For the iPhone 18
series, Genius participates in every lens spec except the variable-aperture upgrade, where it stepped
back because of pricing pressure from Chinese competitors. Management says Genius has the
technical capability and could enter if future specs or design changes improve the opportunity.

Genius offers a combination of an established earnings base from smartphone lens and potential
exposure to the passive components required for optical interconnects. We remain constructive on
the lens business and see CPO as a potentially material addition from 2028. The component strategy
provides several routes to customers, but the valuation should reflect uncertainty over qualified
content, allocation and margins. Our Buy rating and NT$1,250 PT is based  on NT$745 base business

(15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) plus NT$505 for the CPO earnings (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in
2028E). The CPO option for Genius is US$1.8bn vs. US$7bn implied in our Largan NT$5,000 PT.
Genius share gains are showing in reported sales, with YTD sales +10% YoY vs. +2% YoY for Largan.
The company is also participating in every spec except variable aperture.  For CPO, Genius delivered
samples of its prism, V-groove, and MT ferrule to FOCI at the end of May, with initial feedback broadly

on track and no major issues reported to date. Our checks indicate the company is also positioning to
supply one of the leading FAU module suppliers for NVIDIA's CPO program from H227.
At NT$1,060, the shares trade at 24.1x our 2027E core EPS and 21.5x our 2028E core EPS, factoring
some of the CPO opportunity in the multiple rather than in estimates relative to the 10 year average
17x P/E. Applying a 15x valuation to the smartphone lens business + 40x to the CPO option from our

scenario of NT$9.30 EPS in 2028E yields upside from the last close at NT$1,060 to a NT$1,250 share
price.

Core business intact: periscope gains offset muted smartphone demand
September sales of NT$2.67bn (−2% MoM, +1% YoY) took Q326 to NT$7.82bn (+38%
QoQ, +1% YoY), in-line with VA consensus. Genius grew 10% YoY in 9M26 against 2%
at Largan, which we attribute to share gains in periscope lenses for premium iPhones,
offsetting legacy price cuts and lower VR lens shipments. We remain 7% below VA on
4Q26E revenue (NT$7.34bn, −6% QoQ) reflecting muted iPhone sales and no content

in the iPhone 18 Pro's new variable-aperture lens.

With CPO revenue contribution <5% in 2026 per management guidance, Genius' lens
business should remain its primary earnings anchor. With foldables shipping in 2H, Q3

shipment orders are broadly in line with historical patterns but should be watchful on
near-term demand trends as customers tend to adjust orders quickly in response to new
product sell-through evidenced by Largan's recent cuts. VR contribution has declined
from 5–10% in 2025 to <5% in 2026 on demand slowdown, partially offsetting
strength in phone lens while the mix shift would enhance Genius' overall margin profile.
On the other hand, AR is seeing traction supported by ongoing Ray-Ban shipments with

next-gen products in development.

### Page 4

Genius position in the CPO value chain
Genius is targeting the CPO market as a component supplier while Largan is advancing
from components into full-FAU assembly. Genius is sampling three components for CPO
with FAU makers, including: 1) high-precision prism to redirect light from the PIC into
the fibers, 2) V-groove/key slot to precisely hold the fibers in place, and 3) MT ferrules
(high density plastic connector bundling and aligning fibers). The companies overlap in

capability, with Largan also having PMLA and fiber arrays but not getting into some
components like MT ferrules and V-Grooves.
Genius's optical design, precision tooling, metrology and volume-manufacturing
experience offer it a good entry point for this market. Commercial differentiation will
depend on reproducible optical performance, dimensional tolerances, thermal stability
and customer yield.  It has announced collaboration with FOCI and  we believe has

potential to also work with one of NVIDIA's leading module suppliers for its H227
program.

Annual FAU TAM reaches US$1.85bn in 2028E and US$4.5bn in 2030E in

our base case
We update our FAU (fiber array unit) TAM factoring CPO/NPO penetration into
AI networking switches, NVIDIA multi-rack systems, scale-up on compute and switch
boards, and non-NVIDIA accelerators.  We assume one passive FAU assembly connecting
a fiber array to a photonic engine with one unit per optical engine.
We assume one FAU per optical engine and 32 per switch ASIC in our base case,
matching NVIDIA's 32x3.2T engines on its 102.4T Spectrum-X package. Broadcom's

102.4Tb/s Davisson uses 16 engines at 6.4Tb/s, so our bear case assumes an even mix of
these two designs (24 per ASIC). We note that higher switch bandwidth does not
automatically increase FAU count if engine bandwidth rises at the same rate. Our 2030E
assumption maintains the 32 FAUs per switch based on bandwidth rising to 204.8Tb/s

Our base case for the FAU market sums up 4 opportunities for CPO/NPO attach:
1. Scale-out and scale-across switches
We estimate annual switch ASIC shipments x CPO + NPO penetration x optical engines
per ASIC x FAUs per engine. In 2028E, 25% CPO/NPO penetration or 1.0m 102.4T
switch ASICs at 32 FAUs each produces 8.0m FAUs. In 2030E, 55% of 1.1mn

204.8T switch ASICs at 32 FAUs drives a market size of 19.4mn FAUs.

2. NVIDIA's multi-rack connections for Rubin Ultra and Feynman
NVIDIA at its 2026 GTC San Jose proposed a multi-rack option to network together 8
Oberon NVL72 racks for the Rubin Ultra generation and 8 Kyber NVL144 racks.

Feedback at the time showed  possible transceiver or NPO connections for Rubin Ultra
and CPO connections for Feynman.  Our 2028 base case uses 110,000 racks, 15% multi-
rack adoption and 75% CPO/NPO penetration, giving approximately 12,400 connected
racks.
For 2030E, our rack count falls to 35,000 racks as we assume Kyber would have 4x the
number of GPUs, equivalent to 140k Oberon equivalents. We assume the 35,000 racks
at 55% multi-rack adoption x 80% CPO/NPO attach gives 15,400 connected racks. We

assume about 216 and 576 inter-rack FAUs per rack, respectively, driving a market
opportunity of 2.7mn FAUs in 2028E and 8.9mn in 2030E.

Overall market opportunity: 12.3mn/35.6mn generates a US$1.85bn/US
$4.5bn TAM in 2028/2030E
We sum four pools: scale-out/scale-across switches, NVIDIA multi-rack pods, CPO native
NVIDIA GPUs and non-NVIDIA scale-up.  We add 10%/5% for FAUs lost at downstream
attach and test and for field spares, driving a market size of in our base case of 12.3mn
FAUs in 2028E and 35.6mn in 2030E. At US$150/US$125 per FAU, that is a US$1.85bn

market in 2028E and US$4.5bn in 2030E (bear/bull US$0.5bn/US$3.5bn in 2028E; US
$1.4bn/US$9.0bn in 2030E). The expansion to 2030E comes from wider optical
adoption and greater content in scale-up systems, despite an assumed FAU ASP decline
from US$150 to US$125.

Scenario analysis on Genius CPO opportunity

### Page 7

We assume in base case Genius captures 10% of the TAM in 2028E and 15% by 2030E.
The base case in 2028E is consistent with US$30 of prism, V-groove and ferrule content
in half of all FAUs or US$15 average content spread across the FAU market.  The
2030E bull case (25%) or US$37.50 would require near full content of some of the sub-
components or otherwise ability to integrate more of the solution value by that time.
Our bear case assumes only 1% share of the opportunity in 2028E and 5% of the

opportunity in 2030E, with EPS even by 2030E falling to NT$4 and yielding limited
valuation potential for the shares.

Reiterate Buy and raise PT to NT$1,250 valuing in CPO optionality

We adjust our valuation method from P/B to P/E SOTP to better reflect the company’s
core business and its opportunity within CPO industry while the core smartphone
business is demonstrating more stable earnings to use P/E vs. prior P/B. Our PT at NT
$1,250 is based on NT$745 base business  (15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) plus NT$505
for the CPO earnings (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in 2028E) with catalysts from
upcoming qualifications into leading FAU makers and potential strategic private

placement with board approving 7mn share private placement (albeit 6% dilution if
issued). We note Genius CPO option valued at US$1.8bn vs. US$7bn implied in our
Largan valuation.
Our price target uses 2028 earnings for both businesses, reflecting the modeled start of
meaningful CPO contribution. We apply 15x core EPS of NT$49.25 and 40x CPO EPS of
NT$12.72 for a NT$1,250 price target. The 40x multiple assumes the optical business
can sustain strong growth after its initial ramp; qualification, concentration and margin

risk limit the confidence that can be placed on that earnings stream.
In the upside case, stronger premium lens participation and manufacturing execution lift
2028E core EPS to NT$54.32. Faster optical adoption expands the 2028E FAU market to
US$3.46bn; 15% effective revenue capture and a 31% pretax margin produce
approximately NT$36.88 CPO EPS. Applying 15x to core earnings and 42x to CPO
earnings gives approximately NT$2,365.
In the downside case, weaker lens demand and margin pressure lower core EPS to NT

$44.29. Slower optical adoption leaves a US$0.46bn FAU market, of which Genius
captures 1%, producing only NT$0.29 CPO EPS. Applying 12x and 38x gives
approximately NT$540. The downside is driven by both a weaker core business and a
much smaller optical contribution.

Company Description
Founded in 1989, Genius Electronic Optical (GSEO) is a leading global manufacturer of
camera lenses, providing full service optical solutions, including mobile lenses, automotive
lenses and AR/VR optics. Its production sites are in Taichung (one plant) and Xiamen (two

plants). The Taichung plant mainly manufactures VR lenses, while the Xiamen plants produce
compact lenses for consumer electronics. Optical lens products accounted for 73% of 2023
total revenue, and others include TOF lenses, flash lenses, VR lenses and LED lighting
products. Its major client is Apple and it supplies lenses for iPhones and iPads.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's

interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of
shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our Genius price target is based on PE-based sum-of-the-parts model. Genius' growth is
highly leveraged to the overall handset industry and could be affected by volatility in handset
demand, as well as whether handset OEMs will continue upgrading their camera design.

Genius' margins could be affected by increasing competition and the rise of new
technologies, ASP pressure from customers and fluctuating material and labour costs.
Downside risks include competitors catching up in technology, yield rate issues for new
products and uncertain iPhone demand.
Our price target is based on SOTP valuation on the CPO opportunity and traditional lens
business. We think Largan's downside risks include: 1) ongoing pricing pressure from

smartphone OEM clients and increasingly intense competition among lens suppliers; and 2) a
slower-thanexpected smartphone demand recovery in China, and 3) CPO commercial success
below the market's elevated expectations. We believe the key upside risk is a faster-than-
expected CPO qualification process at Largan. In addition, if the company demonstrates
exceptionally efficient and highly accurate automation capabilities, it could support greater
market share gains within the industry than we currently anticipate.

horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.
Important Information About Sustainable Investing Strategies: Sustainable investing strategies aim to consider and incorporate environmental, social and governance
(ESG) factors into investment process and portfolio construction. Strategies across geographies approach ESG analysis and incorporate the findings in a variety of ways.
Incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing considerations may inhibit the portfolio manager’s ability to participate in certain investment opportunities that otherwise

would be consistent with its investment objective and other principal investment strategies. The returns on a portfolio incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing
considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

營收走勢圖

研究員/丁宥霖 yulin.ting@uni-psg.com 
       廖婉婷 TINA_LIAO@uni-psg.com

臻鼎 9 月營收 263.58 億元 ， 改寫單月新高，3Q26 營收 646.24 
億元，優於研究部原估的 607.09 億元，主因 AI 相關業務出貨
優於預期 ， 加上手機客戶進入新品旺季 ， 研究部據此上修 3Q26 稅
後 EPS 至 3.84 元（原估 3.47 元）。展望 4Q26，行動通訊旺
季延續， 加上 GPU 與 ASIC 新一代主板開始放量 ，營收與獲利將
續創新高 。2027 年伺服器與光模塊隨新平台出貨擴大及 1.6T 成
為主流， 營收可望各再增逾百億元；IC 載板亦受惠供給持續吃緊
及新產能陸續開出 ， 帶動 AI 相關營收占比突破三成 。 綜上所述 ，
研究部上修臻鼎 2026、2027 年稅後 EPS 至 12.11 元（+99.6% 
YoY，原估 11.81 元）及 26.14 元（+118.6% YoY，原估 22.91 
元）。基於臻鼎產品橫跨軟板、類載板、HDI、HLC 與 IC 載板，
可為客戶提供一站式解決方案 ， 並提前掌握關鍵材料與設備產能 ，
在高階 PCB 供給吃緊下擴產得以大量開出搶奪市占，研究部維
持以 2027 年獲利為評價基礎及 28 倍本益比，目標價上調至 
730 元，維持買進投資評等。

重點摘要： 
1. 9 月營收改寫新高，上修 2H26 獲利預估 
臻鼎 9 月營收 263.58 億元（+27.5% MoM、+36.1% YoY），改寫
單月新高 ， 其中伺服器 、 光模塊與 IC 載板合計營收年增逾三倍 ，
行動通訊亦呈雙位數月增 與年增 。3Q26 營收 646.24 億元
（+33.4% QoQ、+36.4% YoY），達研究部原估的 106.4%，明顯優
於預期 ， 主因 GPU 新一代主板進入量產 、1.6T 光模塊出貨放大 ，
加上手機客戶進入新品旺季。考量 AI 相關產品占比提升，研究
部預估 3Q26 毛利率為 25.5%（+2.4ppt QoQ、+3.5ppt YoY，原
估 24.7%），稅後 EPS 上修至 3.84 元（+81.9% QoQ 、+81.9% 
YoY，原估 3.47 元） 。展望 4Q26，行動通訊旺季延續，加上 GPU 
與 ASIC 新一代主板放量，研究部預估 4Q26 營收 736.13 億元
（+13.9% QoQ、+29.4% YoY），稅後 EPS 4.75 元（+23.8% QoQ、
+70.4% YoY，原估 4.86 元），營收與獲利續創新高。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. AI 伺服器主板成為最主要成長動能 
AI 伺服器主板隨 GPU 與 ASIC 平台升級，晶片封裝尺寸、I/O 
數與傳輸速率同步提升，主板須在有限面積內完成更多高速訊號
的扇出與繞線。 傳統 HLC 以一次壓合搭配機械鑽孔與背鑽 ，層間
連接密度已近極限；高階 HDI 則以多次壓合與雷射盲孔逐層堆
疊，線路更細、訊號路徑更短，可降低高速傳輸損耗，帶動 GPU 
平台 OAM 等主板設計由 HLC 轉向 HDI。HDI 須投入大量雷射鑽
孔機與多次壓合產能，在設備與材料交期拉長下，全球高階  AI 
PCB 產能缺口約 15–20%， 訂單已排至 2028 年，2027–2028 年
產品報價亦將持續上調。臻鼎提早取得全球逾五成高階雷射鑽孔
機產能 ， 在建 22 座新廠中半數投入 HDI、 伺服器與光模塊產品 ，
在同業擴產受限下得以持續承接訂單，帶動伺服器 HDI 比重由 
1H26 的約五成於 2H26 明顯提升；繼 ASIC 專案今年大量出貨
後，GPU 平台新一代主板亦自 4Q26 起放量。研究部預估臻鼎伺
服器營收 2026、2027 年各增加逾 100 億元 ， 為未來兩年最主要
成長動能。

3. 臻鼎可望躍居 1.6T 光模塊 PCB 龍頭 
AI 伺服器傳輸速率由 800G 升級至 1.6T，單通道速率倍增使線
路須更細 、 阻抗控制更精準 ， 傳統減成法已難以達到 ， 光模塊 PCB 
因此改採 mSAP 製程。臻鼎過去為手機類載板主要供應商，具備 
mSAP 大量生產經驗 ； 在高階設備交期拉長 、 關鍵材料供給吃緊下 ，
臻鼎已掌握市場上多數高階設備產能並持續擴充，同時提前鎖定
上游材料，得以在淮安與泰國同步擴充光模塊產能，研究部推估
臻鼎可望在 2027 年 1.6T 世代成為全球光模塊 PCB 龍頭。隨 
1.6T 出貨占比由 2026 年的約五成提升至 2027 年逾七成，研
究部預估臻鼎光模塊營收由 2025 年約 9 億元躍升至 2026 年
逾 100 億元，2027 年再增至逾 250 億元。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

4. ABF 與 BT 同步吃緊，新產能與轉單推升載板營收 
AI 晶片封裝面積與層數持續放大，ABF 載板用量與製程難度同
步提升，惟新廠建置期長、上游材料吃緊，供需缺口持續擴大；
同業亦將部分 BT 產能轉往 ABF，使 BT 載板同步出現缺貨，兩
者報價自下半年起持續上漲。臻鼎 ABF 載板技術規格已達最大 
158×158mm、 最高 28 層 ， 深圳一廠 1Q26 轉盈並將於年底滿載 ，
二廠 1Q27 開始貢獻，第二園區 2028 年起約每年開出一座新
廠，且新產能幾乎全由國際客戶預先包下，高雄廠亦已量產先進
封裝相關載板。BT 載板雖無新建廠計畫， 但近期承接同業轉單並
調漲報價 ， 加上產品與客戶組合持續優化 ， 可望維持雙位數成長 。
隨 ABF 產能接續開出，研究部預估 ABF 占載板營收比重將由 
2025 年的 35% 提升至 2027 年約七成，IC 載板營收 2026、
2027 年分別成長 99.2% 及 89.0%。

5. 高階新產能陸續開出，產品組合優化推升毛利率 
臻鼎 2026 年資本支出逾 800 億元，2027 年維持相當水準，廠
房數將由目前的 28 座擴增至 2030 年底的 59 座。新產能自 
2027 年起陸續開出：淮安 6 座新廠於 2026 年底完工後進入裝
機，HD 園區預計 3M27 投產、2Q27 大批量產；泰國新廠於 4Q26 
起試產，1H27 進入小量生產 ； 深圳第三園區與載板第二園區則預
計自 2028 年起接棒 （表二） 。新廠初期稼動率與良率仍在爬坡，
加上 2027 年折舊費用預計較 2026 年再增加 20% 至 30%，短
期將稀釋毛利率；惟新增產能以 AI 伺服器、光模塊與 IC 載板
為主，隨 AI 相關營收占比由 2025 年的 11.7% 提升至 2027 
年突破三成，研究部預估臻鼎毛利率將由 2026 年的 24.5% 提
升至 2027 年的 29.3%。

6. 上修 2026、2027 年獲利預估 
2026 年 AI 相關業務為主要成長動能 ，研究部預估伺服器 、光模
塊與其他營收成長 269.0%，IC 載板成長 99.2%，行動通訊受惠
摺疊機新品成長 6.9%，電腦消費則與 2025 年持平，AI 相關營
收占比將由 2025 年的 11.7% 提升至 25.9%。研究部預估 2026 
年營收 2,273.96 億元（+24.6% YoY，原估 2,267.70 億元），
毛利率由 24.1% 上修至 24.5%（+4.7ppt YoY ），稅後 EPS 由 
11.81 元上修至 12.11 元（+99.6% YoY）。 
展望 2027 年，伺服器與光模塊隨新平台出貨擴大及 1.6T 成為
主流，營收合計成長逾一倍，IC 載板受惠 AI 客戶訂單放量、
ABF 比重提升及報價調漲，成長 89.0%，行動通訊與電腦消費則
分別維持中、低個位數成長。在高毛利產品占比提升、費用率隨
營收規模擴大回落，抵銷新廠折舊增加下，研究部預估 2027 年
營收 3,158.99 億元（+38.9% YoY，原估 3,083.20 億元），毛
利率提升至 29.3%（+4.8ppt YoY，原估 28.1%） ，營益率提升至 
17.9%（原估 14.8%） ， 稅後 EPS 上修至 26.14 元（+118.6% YoY，
原估 22.91 元）。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

供 專 屬 之 投 資 建 議 。 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何 意 見 ， 不 構 成 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 券 或 其 他 投
資 標 的 之 要 約 、 宣 傳 或 引 誘 等 事 項 。 對 於 本 投 資 報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 券 、 投
資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與

## Document 180: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

HD현대중공업의 3Q26 연결 실적은 매출액 5조 8,602억원(yoy +33%, qoq -7%), 
영업이익 9,161억원(yoy +64%, qoq -12%, OPM 15.6%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 하회할 
전망이다. 동사의 3Q26 조업일수는 여름휴가, 추석연휴 및 중대재해 등 영향으로 
qoq -21% 감소했으며 환율 역시 qoq -84원 감소한 영향을 감안해 전분기 대비 
실적을 보수적으로 추정했다. 그럼에도 해양 부문 Trion FPU발 C/O에 따른 일회성 
환입이 예상됨에 따라 일부 이익 상쇄가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 한편, 동사의 
임단협 부결로 당분기 추가적인 인건비 부담은 제한적일 것으로 판단되며 잠수함 
화재 사고 관련 비용 역시 4Q 이후 반영될 전망이다.

동사는 급격히 커져가는 AIDC향 전력수요 및 발전용 엔진 수주에 대응하고자 9/10 
총 4GW 규모의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 증설을 위한 신규 투자를 공시헸다. 동사의 
기존 DC향 4행정 중속엔진 수주 단가를 고려 시 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 
가능할 전망이며 2행정 및 선박용 4행정 엔진 대비 높은 수익성이 예상되는 바 
생산이 본격화될 28년부터 동사 실적 성장성의 핵심 동인으로 작용할 전망이다.

동사에 대해 투자의견 BUY를 유지하며 업종 밸류에이션 디레이팅 및 시장 프리미엄 
조정에 따른 Target Multipe 변동으로 목표주가를 70만원으로 하향 조정한다. 상선 
시장 수주 경쟁 심화로 업종 밸류 조정이 이루어지고 있으나 동사는 엔진 기반 장기 
성장성으로 Peer 대비 상대적 매력도 확보 및 주가 방어가 가능하다는 판단이다.

## Document 205: Anthropic七位创始人近乎平分股权，IPO后持股价值或高达2000亿美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic七位创始人近乎平分股权，IPO后持股价值或高达2000亿美元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 即将 IPO ，七位联合创始⼈⼏乎平分股权，各⾃持股价值最⾼达 300 亿美元，合
计约 2000 亿美元。这⼀罕⻅平等架构打破硅⾕⾦字塔式薪酬惯例，七⼈还承诺捐出 80% 股
票，但受锁定期约束，最早上市六个⽉后⽅可部分变现。
摘要：
七位创始⼈平分巨额股权，打破硅⾕惯例的薪酬架构，令这场 AI 独⻆兽上市备受瞩⽬。
据 Fortune 分析，Anthropic 即将启动 IPO ，七位联合创始⼈各⾃持有的股权在公司上市后合计
价值最⾼可达 2000 亿美元。若按部分媒体报道所述的 1.5 万亿⾄ 2 万亿美元估值区间计算，每位
创始⼈的持股价值约在 210 亿⾄ 300 亿美元之间。值得关注的是，包括 CEO Dario Amodei 和总裁
兼董事会主席  Daniela Amodei 在内的七⼈，持股数量⼏乎完全相同，彻底打破了⼤型科技公司
普遍采⽤的 " ⾦字塔式 " 薪酬结构。
这⼀架构对投资者⽽⾔意义重⼤。七位创始⼈均被列为 IPO 的售股⽅，但受锁定期约束，最早须

在上市约六个⽉后⽅可出售部分股份，余下股份需待约⼀年后才可变现。此外，七⼈已承诺将
各⾃ 80% 的股票捐作慈善，若兑现，捐赠总额可能⾼达 800 亿⾄ 1650 亿美元，尽管该承诺⽬前不
具法律约束⼒。
近乎平等的股权分配，硅⾕罕⻅先例
据 Fortune 分析，每位联合创始⼈持有的股份数量介于 2.31 亿⾄ 2.44 亿股之间，并且每⼈持有完
全相同的 2,185,400 份可⽴即⾏权的股票期权，以及将在 IPO 后归属的 639,500 份限制性股票单位
（ RSU ）。按此计算，七⼈各⾃约持有公司 1.4% ⾄ 1.5% 的股权，合计持股价值约 2000 亿美元。
薪酬咨询公司 Farient Advisors 创始⼈ Robin Ferracone 表⽰， Anthropic 对创始团队采取的 " 平等
主义⽅式 " ⼗分罕⻅，七⼈的创始团队规模本⾝也属 " 相对较⼤ " 。 " 给所有创始⼈相同的东西，这
本⾝就不寻常， " 她说， " 通常随着归属周期推进，薪酬会逐渐与职位挂钩⽽产⽣分化。 "

Anthropic 在周⼀提交的招股说明书中将这⼀创始团队描述为 " 独特具备使命管理能⼒的群体 " ，
同时特别指出 Dario 和 Daniela 对于 " 制定公司战略⽅向⾄关重要 " ，且 " 难以替代 " 。
Amodei 兄妹：同等薪酬，同步晋升
招股⽂件显⽰，由于两位 Amodei 是公司认定的仅有的执⾏官，其薪酬信息得以完整披露。去
年，兄妹⼆⼈各⾃获得 654,167 美元的年薪，加上⽂件中估值约合计 1300 万美元的股票和期权
—— 若按 2 万亿美元估值折算，则价值约 3.3 亿美元。今年 7 ⽉，两⼈均获加薪，年薪升⾄ 140 万
美元。
今年 2 ⽉， Anthropic 向两⼈各授予约 140 万份股票期权及 240 万份 RSU ， 7 ⽉再追加 53.7 万份
RSU 。上述 RSU 将在 Anthropic 上市后以季度为单位分批归属，前提是两⼈仍在职。按 2 万亿美
元估值计算，上述授予对两⼈⽽⾔各价值约 5.05 亿美元。
公司在注册声明中解释道： " 我们在制定⾼管薪酬时会将内部薪酬平等纳⼊考量。每位联合创始

⼈ 获 得 相 同 的 薪 酬 。 " 其 余 五 位联 合 创 始 ⼈ — — To m  B r o w n 、 J a r e d  K a p l a n 、 S a m
McCandlish 、 Jack Clark 和 Chris Olah—— 均持有⾸席计算官、⾸席科学官等 C 级职位，但未出
现在公司薪酬表中。不过，七⼈持有完全相同数量的期权和 RSU ，归属时间均不晚于 11 ⽉底，
与已披露的两位 Amodei 的授予情况⾼度吻合，暗⽰其余五⼈获得了同等授予。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
图⽚
与⻢斯克模式的鲜明对⽐
这⼀结构与 SpaceX 的薪酬安排形成强烈反差。 SpaceX 是上⼀家以类似规模上市的公司，在
2026 年 6 ⽉ IPO 前的数⽉内，⻢斯克共获得总计 13 亿股限制性股票，分两批授予，均与市值⾥程
碑挂钩，其中⼀批与⽕星移⺠相关，另⼀批与太空数据中⼼相关。若⽬标全部实现，上述授予
价值可能⾼达 6500 亿⾄ 7000 亿美元，公司其他任何⼈与之⽆从⽐较。
相⽐之下， Anthropic 七位创始⼈承诺将 80% 的股票捐作慈善，且其持股架构使他们在捐出⼤部
分财富后仍能维持对公司的控制权。
值得注意的是，两位 Amodei 此前已在今年 4 ⽉的要约收购中以每股 25.91 美元的价格出售了部分
股份。
监督薪酬架构的董事会成员本⾝亦是投资⽅

Anthropic IPO 后负责监督⾼管薪酬的两位董事会成员，均为该公司的投资⼈。诺华 CEO Vas
Narasimhan 担任 Anthropic 薪酬委员会主席，他在今年 5 ⽉加⼊董事会数周后，通过其与妻⼦共
同持有的公司购⼊约 1000 万美元的 Anthropic 股票。薪酬委员会另⼀名成员 Yasmin Razavi 是
Spark Capital 的普通合伙⼈，其旗下基⾦⾃ 2023 年以来已累计向 Anthropic 投资 7.77 亿美元。
Anthropic 尚未为 IPO 定价，具体细节仍可能发⽣变化。

## Document 233: Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-27)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-27).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
10:46 October 16 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised
to the downside, -39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force participation from August’s near-
historic high largely offset. The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August was revised up.

Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless, the take home point from the update is that the

### Page 2

labour market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs below working-age population growth.
We expect the unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter financial conditions and cost-of-
living pressures restrict activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast peak is near at
4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the communication challenge for the RBA. The
September minutes were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing on materialising

upside risks to inflation. But, with the unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption of
4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the labour market is becoming harder to defend.
Still, with a clear focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our near-term CPI forecasts in
the October Market Outlook – we still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in September, taking the annual rate up to

3.7%yr. Energy prices were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation was a touch above
consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr). Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months (0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4%
(3.1%yr) on a 0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport services and a 0.3%
increase in medical care services (2.7%yr and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release of

the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures, with the headline and core measures up
6.3%yr and 5.0%yr respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these developments, firms
across multiple districts citing tariffs, insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost
pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect further price increases as contracts/hedges
roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is enough signal in these reports to warrant a

follow-up hike at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee members believe they are
near the end of the tightening cycle and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman both
showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear bias towards an October or December decision.
Williams stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in this cycle given known information.
And Waller also showed confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are doing some of the

FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating
that one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation near the 2.0%yr medium-term target.
However, as discussed in this month’s Market Outlook, capacity pressures are likely to see inflation
consistently above target, rather than below, in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity
in the housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to remain modestly restrictive over

the forecast horizon and will also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade account. At USD133.5bn, the September
result was a whisker inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More impressively, throughout
2025 and 2026 year-to-date the surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the average of
2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth of export growth is as impressive as the headline

annual gain of 36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese investment in
neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time, both trade and income receipts from offshore
investment should buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound foreign investment should
moderate the pace of gains against other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.

### Page 3

Related articles
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the evolution of producer prices across
Asia, and the likelihood of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent decades
continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was one of the key drivers of declining goods prices.
While still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing development to instead be driven by
capital, technology and energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking production are

also increasingly prioritising resilience, security and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore
more probably future supply-chain developments will help contain rising costs through productivity than
drive sustained price declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.

## Document 237: Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds

Source PDF: all report pdf/Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Compelling risk-reward in 
US 10-year bonds  
Summary 
Event: The US 10 -year government bond yield has surged 
past 5.0% to a 19-year high and has now entered our three -
month forecast range of 5.0-5.25%. At the time of writing, the 
10-year yield is trading at 5.2%, its highest since June 2007’s 
high of 5.32%. The next resistance levels are the 2002 high 
of 5.47% and the 2001 high of 5.53%.  
We view the risk/reward for the US 10-year government 
bond after the recent yield spike as  compelling and we 
are initiating a long opportunistic idea at the current 
yield. As the table on the right shows, if the 10 -year yield 
rises to 6%, investors would face less than 1% in negative 
total returns over a one-year period, whereas if the yield falls 
below 5.0%, they would gain at least 6% in total returns.  
While we continue to prefer the 3-7-year bond maturity range 
for income generation (the US 5-year bond yield is also 
above 5%), and broadly prefer corporate over government 
bonds amid solid corporate fundamentals, the US 10 -year 
government bond yield has finally reached reasonable levels 
for investors looking for slightly longer maturities. 
Rationale: We expect the US 10-year government bond yield 
to ease to 4.75-5.0% over a 12-month period due to: 
Easing oil prices and inflation over next 12 months: While 
the yield is likely to remain elevated in the next three months 
due to elevated oil prices caused by geopolitical uncertainty, 
heavy AI investment-driven bond issuance and a resilient US 
economy, it is likely to ease towards the 4.75 -5.0% range 
over 12  months as oil prices moderate either due to a 
resolution in the Middle East or a rerouting of Middle East 
energy supplies and as the impact of tariffs fade.  
We expect s hallower Fed rate hikes than market s: We 
expect a total of 50bps of Fed rate hikes by June 2027, before 
a cut in H2 as US inflation eventually eases towards the Fed’s 
2% target. This  rate trajectory is shallower than c. 95bps of 
further hikes priced by markets over the next 12 months. 
 The benchmark US 10-year government bond yield has 
risen to its highest since 2007, offering medium-term 
investors a compelling risk-reward trade-off 
US 10-year government bond yield and previous highs

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Investor positioning adjustment: In the very near-term, the 
US 10 -year bond yield has room to move lower as hedge 
funds using systematic strategies (CTAs) are extremely short 
US government bonds, raising the chance of short -covering 
if upcoming jobs and inflation data miss estimates. 
A shallow Fed hiking cycle  
We expect the shallowest Fed hiking cycle in almost 
three decades. This should enable the US 10-year yield to 
ease towards 4.75 -5.0% over a 12 -month period. Following 
this month’s increase, we forecast two further 25bp hikes by 
June 2027, followed by a cut in H2 2027. Markets price at 
least three more hikes by June and a strong probability of a 
fourth by September. Our projected 75bp s of total hikes this 
cycle compares with 525bps of hikes in 2022–23, 225bps in 
2015–18, 425bps in 2004–06 and 175bp in 1999–2000. The 
last smaller mid-cycle move was a single 25bps hike in 1997. 
Why we expect a more cautious Fed 
The latest hike restored the Fed’s credibility after renewed 
oil-price pressures, elevated inflation and a balanced labour 
market prompted a unanimous policy vote. However, the 
following factors argue against aggressive tightening: 
Supply-driven inflation: Energy costs and AI-infrastructure 
constraints, rather than excess demand, are driving prices. 
Higher rates cannot repair supply and may weaken growth. 
Labour is no longer the main inflation source:  Labour 
supply and demand are better balanced, wage growth has 
moderated and unemployment is near full employment. AI 
further weakens the traditional Phillips Curve relationship 
between unemployment and inflation. 
Disinflation should resume:  Energy pressures may fade 
after the US mid-term elections, while the one-off inflationary 
effects of tariffs should diminish after 2025–26. 
AI raises the neutral rate , but dampens inflation:  
Investment and productivity may lift the neutral rate ( r*), the 
rate consistent with stable inflation and full employment, 
while efficiency gains limit wage-price spirals. 
Markets are already tightening  financial conditions : 
Higher bond yields have raised borrowing costs and 
restrained activity, doing part of the Fed’s work. 
The cycle began from a higher base:  The Fed funds rate 
started at 3.75%, versus 0.25% in the previous two cycles 
and 1% before that, reducing additional tightening required. 
Monetary transmission has weakened:  Wealthier 
households drive more spending but are less rate -sensitive, 
while lower -income consumers are already constrained. 
Further hikes may , therefore, curb demand less effectively 
while causing disproportionate harm to low-income families. 
 We expect a shallower Fed rate hiking cycle compared 
with market expectations

Small business conditions are deteriorating:  Higher 
financing and energy costs are weakening sentiment, as 
reflected in the latest Beige Book . The renewed 
underperformance of US small capitalisation (S&P 600) and 
mid capitalisation (S&P 400) equity indices  versus the large 
cap S&P 500 index over the past couple of months also show 
rising pressure on small businesses. 
Fiscal and financial -stability risks are rising:  Aggressive 
Fed tightening would increase US debt-servicing costs as the 
US Treasury seeks cheaper funding through shorter-maturity 
issuance, while potentially amplifying financial-system stress. 
Upside risk to bond yields 
Our view of lower bond yields carries the following risks: 
Persistent inflation:  Repeated supply shocks, strong AI -
related investment demand and tighter immigration controls 
could keep inflation above target and force steeper Fed rate 
hikes. With unemployment near the Fed’s estimate of full 
employment, a sharp drop in labour supply could intensify 
wage pressures and destabilise inflation expectations . We 
note that long-term expectations remain contained for now. 
Geopolitical escalation:  An intensification of geopolitical 
conflicts after the mid -term elections could lift energy prices 
and inflation. However, a Democrat -controlled or divided 
Congress may constrain US foreign-policy initiatives through 
tighter budgets, while Republicans could turn less supportive 
as attention shifts to the 2028 presidential race. 
Higher neutral rate: AI investments could raise the neutral 
rate (r*), turning current monetary policy settings 
accommodative. This could necessitate further rate hikes. 
However, the Fed has barely raised its nominal neutral rate 
estimate, lifting it to 3.2% in September from 3.0% a year ago. 
Bottomline: The outlook for rates remains geopolitics 
dependent, with all Fed meetings live . However, based on 
our core scenario, we see the US 10-year yield settling lower 
in the 4.75-5.0% in 6-12 months. A shallow Fed hiking cycle 
due to expected disinflation offers investors a reasonably 
attractive risk-reward in US 10-year government bonds. 
US 30-year government bonds , on the other hand, may 
continue to see elevated yields as the US fiscal deficit is 
unlikely to have improved next year, and may look much 
worse. As a result, “term premium” may keep the ultra -long 
end elevated. Short of some unorthodox policy to artifici ally 
cap them lower, the 30-year yield is likely to continue to drift 
higher towards the 5.5-5.75% range into H2 2027. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist 
Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

## Document 253: El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

30 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EL NIÑO 2026–2027: MULTIPLE PRESSURES ON PRICES AND SUPPLIES
Hélène Drouot
The 2026–2027 El Niño climate event is expected to be one of the most intense in the last fifty years, with sea-surface-temperature
anomalies potentially exceeding +3°C in the equatorial Pacific. According to the World Meteorological Organisation (WMO), El Niño
is expected to persist until February 2027, with asymmetrical effects, as South-East Asia and northern Latin America will experience

droughts, while southern Brazil and Argentina could see heavy rainfall. Above all, El Niño is compounding the effects of global warming
and exacerbating existing extreme-weather events (droughts, floods and heatwaves). The economic effects of this climate shock mainly
play out through three avenues: agricultural commodity prices, energy and logistics (see heatmap).
Agricultural commodity prices: volatility is causing tensions, with inflationary effects

In India and South-East Asia, droughts will affect harvests, pushing up prices (particularly for coffee, cocoa, rice and palm oil). Conversely, in
southern Brazil and Argentina, heavy rainfall will boost soya and maize production, putting downward pressure on prices. According to the IMF,
El Niño causes global food prices to rise by between 5% (in a typical episode) and 8% (very high intensity) year-on-year, with the full impact being
felt 6 to 12 months after the climatic peak.

Therefore, for a given country, the weighting of food in its inflation index (CPI) plays a decisive role. As a result, all other things being equal, an 8%
rise in prices adds around 1 pp to inflation when food accounts for 13% of the CPI basket (average for countries shown in green on the heatmap),
1.8 pp when this share reaches 22% (yellow), 2.8 pp at 35% (orange) and nearly 4 pp above 49% (red).
The risks are skewed to the upside, as the 8% estimate does not take into account additional losses linked to droughts, floods and other extre -

me events, while policy responses to shocks (stockpiling by importers and increased export restrictions) may amplify and prolong price pres -
sures. The rice market between 2023 and 2024 illustrates this well, as the combination of rice-export restrictions imposed by India in July 2023
(in order to protect its domestic market) and of the drought in South-East Asia contributed to a 30% average increase in the price of rice over the
course of 2023 and kept global prices high for several months after the end of the El Niño episode.

Energy production sector: heavy reliance on hydroelectricity leaves the sector vulnerable to a second supply shock
Droughts will reduce hydroelectric power generation in Latin America and South-East Asia. As a result, some countries will have to turn to other,
more expensive solutions, such as fossil fuels, against a backdrop of already high oil and gas prices. This shift will increase global demand for
oil and put pressure on prices which, according to the IMF, could persist for up to four quarters after the initial El Niño shock. For example,

in Colombia, where hydropower accounts for 55% of electricity generation, a 30% drop in output (already seen in 2023–2024) could increase the
cost of electricity generation by 15 to 20% (assuming that the cost of thermal power generation is twice as high as hydropower). In Ecuador (where
hydropower accounts for 70% of electricity generation) and Vietnam (30%), electricity shortages are likely to disrupt local industries.
SOURCE: IMF, WORLD BANK, EMBER, BNP PARIBAS CALCULATIONS

Vulnerabilities differ, depending on a country’s dependence on food, energy and logistics

Reading note: Scores are standardised on a scale of 0 to 10 for ease of comparison between countries and supply channels.
- Food vulnerability: based on the weight of food in the CPI.
- Energy vulnerability: based on the share of hydropower in electricity generation.
- Logistics vulnerability: the ‘Trade and transport-related infrastructure’ component of the World Bank’s Logistics Perfor-

mance Index, which measures the quality of the infrastructure required for transport and international trade.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 30 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
Logistics: transport infrastructure is another point of vulnerability
The final channel relates to disruptions to freight transport against a backdrop of already weakened value chains. The variable used in our
heatmap is a proxy for economies’ abilities to absorb a shock (particularly a climate-related one) affecting their transport infrastructure (for

example, by maintaining traffic flow, diverting or reorganising transport flows). The heatmap highlights the particularly high vulnerability
of Latin America. Its capacity to absorb a logistics shock is relatively limited, and there is significant exposure at a strategic chokepoint, the
Panama Canal, through which approximately 5% of global trade passes. Low rainfall in the canal basin has already led to the number of
daily transits being reduced in September 2026 (as in 2023–2024); further restrictions are expected in October (to 29 transits per day, down

from 36 in August), which will increase transport times and costs. Europe is also being affected, with low water levels on the Rhine and the
Danube already disrupting river transport and electricity generation.
Latin America and South-East Asia are hit hardest
Therefore, the economies most at risk are not necessarily those that will suffer the greatest climate shock, but those where multiple trans -
mission channels converge, including a high weighting of food prices in inflation, reliance on hydroelectricity, fragile transport infrastructure

and limited fiscal room for manoeuvre. Latin America and South-East Asia are home to some of the most vulnerable countries, particularly
due to their dependence on food prices and hydroelectricity. Colombia and Ecuador are noteworthy for their high exposure to energy risk,
while Ghana, India and the Philippines are some of the economies most vulnerable to a food shock. Latin America faces a combination of
vulnerabilities, such as high exposure to food and energy price shocks in some countries, which are compounded by logistics risks, as currently

illustrated by the restrictions on traffic through the Panama Canal.
helene.drouot@bnpparibas.com

## Document 318: Persistent energy pressures have further to run (2026-10-27)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Persistent energy pressures have further to run (2026-10-27).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-27

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
MIDDLE EAST
BULLETIN
Persistent energy pressures have further to run
19 October 2026
Key points
• Shipping disruptions, depleted
inventories and elevated freight
costs are keeping physical crude
and fuel markets tight despite a

reported recovery in Gulf exports.
• Brent is forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing
to US$77/bbl in Q4 2027.
• Refined product markets are
expected to ease more slowly than
crude, with elevated gasoil cracks,
shipping and insurance costs slowing
declines in petrol and diesel prices.
• Under an escalation scenario,
renewed conflict and deeper supply
disruptions could push Brent towards
US$150/bbl in H1 2027, prolonging
elevated fuel and LNG prices.

• Under an early resolution scenario, a
durable US-Iran agreement accelerates
supply recovery, taking Brent towards
US$65/bbl by Q4 2027 and bringing
fuel and LNG prices down faster.

### Page 2

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Low inventories and higher shipping costs will keep oil and
fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by Q4 2027
as supply recovers. Refined fuels and LNG prices should

ease more slowly. Risks are two-sided: escalation could
push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply with
prices returning close to pre-conflict levels by end-2027.
This article was first published in the October Market Outlook .
Brent crude is back above US$100/bbl on fading confidence
in a lasting US-Iran agreement and intermittent attacks on
shipping. Buffers remain depleted, with the IEA estimating

global observed inventories were 507mb lower at the end of
August than at the start of the conflict.
This comes despite reported crude exports through the Strait of
Hormuz recovering to around 70% to 90% of pre-conflict levels
in late September. Saudi and UAE exports through the Yanbu and
Fujairah pipelines continue, with the East-West pipeline back to
normal after the early September attacks. Tanker crossings have
risen, although transponders remain switched off, leaving visible

transit at 1% to 4% of pre-conflict levels, while cargo transfers in
the Gulf of Oman have increased as an alternative route.
However, these workarounds are slower and more costly. Ship-
to-ship transfers can take 5 to 10 days, US escorts through
the Gulf via a route close to the Oman shoreline cause delays
while some tankers are taking longer routes. Fewer ships are
also available, with many redeployed to other markets. Higher
insurance premiums are adding to costs, with Gulf-to-China

tanker rates above US$0.9mn a day, around 15x higher than a
year ago. With prompt barrels (available for physical delivery)
scarce, the gap between physical and futures prices widened
again, with Dated Brent around US$136/bbl in early October
against US$101/bbl for front-month futures.
Oil and fuel prices push higher ...
Refined prices have risen more than crude
Refined product markets were already tight going into the
conflict. Reduced Gulf supply, exacerbated by Ukrainian strikes

on Russian refineries, has added to that tightness. Together
with higher shipping costs and rising Dated Brent prices,
Singapore Gasoil 10ppm (diesel) has roughly doubled since the
Strait closed, compared with around 40% for crude futures.
Consequently, Singapore gasoil crack spread widened
to an average of around US$36.1/bbl in early October,
from US$17.5/bbl across 2025. Alongside the Australian
government’s excise tax returning to normal, after it had been

halved, this has lifted national diesel and petrol prices to
around A$2.83/L and A$2.41/L.
Outlook
We do not see a lasting US-Iran agreement until at least
mid-2027. In the meantime, we assume there will not be a
return to full-scale military intervention. However, periodic
Iranian attacks on ships and facilities are expected to continue,
as will the US blockade of Iranian exports, keeping a supply
risk premium in place. We look for Brent futures to average

around US$101/bbl in Q4, then ease gradually as demand
from refiners and governments to rebuild stocks partly offsets
recovering supply. As the premium fades and the market
moves into surplus in late 2027, we forecast Brent at around
US$77/bbl in Q4 2027, easing towards US$70/bbl in 2028 as
Middle East production fully recovers.
Oil balance to gradually improve over 2027
Global oil supply is expected to rise by 4.0mb/d (4.0%) to
103.7mb/d in 2027, following a 6.1% fall in 2026. Most of

this reflects a gradual recovery in Gulf output, with Houthi
Sian Fenner, Head of Business & Industry Economics
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Luka Belobajdic, Economist

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
attacks on Red Sea shipping keeping Saudi production below
its pre-conflict level until H2 2027. Meanwhile, we look for UAE
production to continue to improve over 2027 and lift above
pre-conflict levels from 2028, as it is no longer constrained by

OPEC quotas. We do not expect a meaningful return of Iranian
oil until an agreement is reached, and damage sustained during
the conflict means production will take time to recover.
We expect global oil demand to be 103.7mb/d in 2026, 0.8mb/d
(0.7%) higher than in 2025, and to rise a further 1.9mb/d (1.8%)
to 105.6mb/d in 2027. Chinese crude imports will continue to
recover over the coming months as refiners restock ahead of
Lunar New Year, but we expect only a gradual return to pre-

conflict levels, as the electrification of transport limits the
recovery in Chinese demand. Globally, demand for diesel and jet
fuel recovers as transport and aviation activity picks up, although
some demand destruction and efficiency gains are expected
to be permanent. The conflict has also highlighted strategic
vulnerabilities, particularly across Asia, so we expect a push to
rebuild reserves adding around 0.5% to demand over 2027.
Fuel prices to ease more slowly than crude

Refined product supply is expected to recover more slowly
than crude, with Gulf refinery runs not returning to pre-war
levels until at least late 2027. Outside the Gulf, there has
been some constructive news. The US has lifted sanctions
on Russian diesel until April 2027, with Russia saying it will
supply more than 4Mt over the coming months, while China
has lifted its ban on refined product exports. However, we
are cautious about how much this will add to the market in

the short term. Much of the Russian supply is headed to the
US and will depend on Russia’s ability to restore refining and
export capacity, while doubts remain over the Russia-Ukraine
energy ceasefire announced by President Trump. China still
manages exports through quotas, which are likely to increase
only gradually as domestic stocks are rebuilt. Refinery runs
should lift ahead of Lunar New Year, but any increase in
exports will take time to reach regional markets.

With product supply recovering slowly and depleted inventories
being rebuilt, Singapore gasoil crack is likely to stay above its
long-run average even as Brent eases, narrowing from US$71/
bbl in Q4 to US$27/bbl by Q4 2027. With Singapore Gas 10ppm
prices, shipping and insurance costs to remain elevated and
easing only gradually in 2027, we forecast Australian diesel
to retail at an average of 274¢/L in Q4, easing to 200¢/L in Q4
2027, and petrol at 230¢/L and 187¢/L respectively.

LNG prices supported through 2027
Japanese LNG prices surged 15.2%mth in September,
averaging US$25.4/MMBtu as supply disruptions in Qatar
and its inability to divert exports around the Strait of Hormuz
tightened markets and intensified competition between
importers. While prices are now more than double pre-conflict
levels, demand destruction across Asia is providing some
offset to tight supply
Our Q4 forecast for Japanese LNG is now US$24.5/mmbtu and

we expect the market to remain structurally tight, with supply
recovery likely to be gradual and prices not returning to pre-
conflict levels until around 2028. Still, Australia’s domestic
gas market should remain insulated, supported by the FY2027
Federal Budget’s domestic gas reservation scheme, which
requires exporters to supply the domestic market with the
equivalent of up to 20% of gas exports. So far this year,
despite the increase in LNG spot prices in Asia, Australian

natural gas prices have remained under the $12 per GJ cap
that has proposed by the Commonwealth government. We are
expecting this to continue well into 2027.
Risks around our oil price outlook are two-sided
Talks are still ongoing in the background, with the US and
Iran exchanging proposals to reopen the Strait, but the gap
between them remains wide. The conflict has also expanded
to the Red Sea and, with inventories sharply drawn down,
there is a smaller buffer to absorb a further shock.

Using the Oxford Economics model, we assess an escalation
scenario, where energy markets face a deeper and more
persistent disruption as the conflict in the Middle East
intensifies. In this case, Brent futures rise to around US$150/
bbl in H1 2027, only dipping to below US$100/bbl in Q2 2028.
We also consider an early resolution scenario, where shipping
and Gulf supply normalise faster, returning Brent to near pre-
conflict levels by end-2027.
Escalation scenario

In the escalation scenario we assume:
Talks stall and the conflict escalates:  the gap between the
US and Iran proves too wide and the US resumes strikes after
the midterms. Iran responds with attacks on shipping and Gulf
energy infrastructure, damaging export, storage and refining
facilities. A lasting agreement is not reached until 2028.
Export routes are disrupted: more frequent attacks curtail
dark crossings of the Strait and ship-to-ship transfers in the

Gulf of Oman. Houthi attacks also cut off Saudi alternative
routes, forcing Gulf producers to cut output and Middle
East crude exports sharply lower. New routes and shipping
arrangements eventually support a recovery in Gulf flows, but
trade patterns take time to adjust.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Product supply fails to recover:  The Russia–Ukraine energy
ceasefire does not hold and strikes on Russian refineries
continue, limiting how much diesel Russia can export,
even with the US sanctions waiver extended. China also

reintroduces its export ban to protect domestic supply.
Oil prices lift to around US$150/bbl in H1 2027: With little
buffer to absorb the supply loss, competition for physical
delivery intensifies, with physical prices well above futures
as prompt barrels remain scarce. Higher prices support
supply growth outside OPEC and drive demand destruction,
alongside further emergency stock releases, but a deeper
and more prolonged global deficit remains. Brent stays above

US$100/bbl until mid-2028 as governments rebuild reserves.
The Singapore gasoil crack widens and, with freight and
insurance costs also higher, Australian diesel and petrol prices
average around 247 cpl and 231 cpl at end 2027. Japanese
LNG also remains elevated around US$23.2/MMBtu in Q4
2027, $9.1 above our baseline.
Early resolution scenario
In the early resolution scenario we assume:
A durable agreement: The US and Iran agree terms to reopen
the Strait by the end of year, and, unlike the June interim deal,

the agreement holds, with progress towards a permanent
settlement in early 2027. Attacks on shipping cease and the
US blockade is lifted, allowing Iranian oil back into the market,
initially from storage. Vessels are quickly repositioned to the
Gulf, with freight and war-risk insurance easing from early
2027 rather than mid-year.
A faster recovery in supply:  With export routes secure, Gulf
production and damaged refinery and export capacity recover

sooner than in the baseline. Outside the Gulf, the US extends
its waiver on Russian diesel beyond April 2027 and China
raises export quotas as domestic stocks are rebuilt.
Demand settles below pre-conflict trend: Some demand
destruction and efficiency gains persist, particularly in China.
With inventories rebuilding faster, government restocking is
smaller and more gradual than in the baseline.
Prices fall quickly, but restocking provides a floor:  Brent

Brent futures ease to around US$88/bbl in Q1 2027 as the
premium for prompt physical barrels unwinds. As the market
moves into balance, Brent eases to around US$65/bbl by Q4
2027, close to pre-conflict levels.
Refined product prices ease faster on lower freight costs with
the Singapore gasoil crack narrowing. Australian diesel and
petrol prices fall to around 182 cpl and 170 cpl by Q4 2027. Gas
markets also ease, although damage to Qatar’s gas facility means

export capacity recovers gradually. Japanese LNG falls to around
US$11.9/MMBtu by end-2027, $2.1 below our baseline.
Economic impact
In the escalation scenario, higher fuel costs flow through to
transport, food and other energy-intensive goods such as
plastics and fertilisers. Australian headline CPI remains above
4.5% for much of 2027, with underlying inflation remaining
sticky at above 3% until late 2028. The RBA faces a difficult
trade off between much higher inflation and a significant

hit to activity, as households cut discretionary spending and
businesses defer investment. Higher LNG export earnings
provide only a moderate offset. In the early resolution scenario,
headline inflation is around 0.1ppt below the baseline in 2027.
New Zealand is more exposed in both scenarios, with food
and energy a larger share of its CPI and no offsetting energy
exports. Headline inflation is around 1ppt higher in the
escalation scenario in Q1 2027.

... the upside presents a more dramatic shift in prices

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

India: RBI monetary policy committee 
meeting (7-Oct)

The macro environment has undergone 
significant changes since the RBI 
monetary policy committee’s (MPC) 
August policy meeting, with global oil 
prices rising past $100bl, and US Fed 
kickstarting their rate hike cycle. 
Domestically, core and headline 
continued to rise, laying the ground for 
inflation to quicken beyond 5.8 -6.0% in 
the second half as price risks broaden. 
Strong 1QFY27 GDP growth meanwhile 
pointed to the economy’s relative 
resilience to external shocks.

Besides fuel feeding into food costs, 
weather has also proved to be disruptive, 
with indications that the El Niño 
phenomenon could extend into 2027. 
International food benchmarks, including 
the UN Food Price index and the 
Bloomberg spot agricultural indice s, 
continue to climb. India’s food inflation 
has become more broad -based, shifting 
beyond vegetables and pulses to sugar, 
milk, protein groups, and edible oils. Sub-
par weather has contributed to this 
upmove, besides idiosyncratic factors 
like swing in production trends (cobweb), 
diversion for fuel usage etc.

After two years of normal monsoon, 
cumulative southwest rainfall this year is 
set to end with rainfall around 12 -13% 
below the long -term average, the most 
since 2015 and resulting in a slower 
build-up in reservoir levels.  
Global benchmark oil price gauge has 
averaged ~$93pb in FY27 year -to-date, 
up 36% yoy  vs FY26. Retail pump fuel 
prices were adjusted in quick succession 
in May26 and have been left unchanged 
since then, while other fuel products 
including CNG, aviation fuel, LPG etc 
have continued to climb. Add to this, 
other pockets like telecom, restaura nts, 
business services, transport fares etc 
have been inching up, reflecting early 
signs of broadening in price pressures (on 
sequential basis). A gradual broadening 
of price pressures is likely to keep 
headline inflation high and narrow the 
real rate buffer significantly.

Authorities are also conducting liquidity 
absorption measures through market 
operations like VRRR, open market 
operations and sell-buy FX swaps. This is 
likely to align the overnight call money 
rates to the benchmark repo rate, after 
having fallen sharply d ue to the surplus 
in the banking system.  Liquidity and core 
inflation have historically displayed a 
modest positive relationship, as per our 
study. As such, the RBI's inflation 
mandate has likely reinforced the push to 
drain excess liquidity.

We expect the RBI MPC to raise the repo 
rate by 25bp to 5.5% , citing pipeline 
inflationary pressures. Stance could be 
changed to withdrawal of 
accommodation to signal hawkish intent. 
We expect one more hike within 4Q26, 
though the odds of additional hikes have 
risen as oil prices stay stubbornly high 
and weather remains unfavourable.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 2, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Malaysia

We expect industrial production to grow 
by 5.8% yoy in August from 4.7% yoy in 
July. Strength in  export-oriented 
manufacturing is likely to persist , 
supported by sustained demand for 
artificial intelligence-related electronics 
goods, while mining output may have 
recovered from its contraction in July.

Taiwan

September trade and inflation data are 
forthcoming. Export growth is expected 
to sustain the 40% pace seen in the 
previous month. The global AI boom is 
facing rising headwinds from 
synchronised rate hikes by G3 central 
banks, higher bond yields, and rising  
financing costs. That said, there are so 
far no clear signs of a significant 
slowdown, based on the high -frequency 
indicators we track across AI demand, 
investment, and supply.

CPI is expected to rebound to 2.4% YoY 
in September, up from 2.0% in August. 
The earlier arrival of the Mid -Autumn 
Festival this year may have boosted food 
and services prices on a YoY basis. The 
rebound in global energy prices should 
also have lifted tran sport costs, 
including gasoline prices and airfares. 
While electricity tariffs will remain 
frozen, the December review implies a 
possibility of an electricity price hike 
after the local elections.

Given still -strong economic growth, 
persistent inflation, and the potential for 
inflation expectations to rise, we 
maintain our view that the central bank 
will raise the policy rate by a modest 
12.5bps to 2.125% in December.

Thailand

We expect Thailand’s headline inflation 
to accelerate to 3.2% yoy in September 
from 2.5% yoy  in August, slightly 
exceeding the central bank’s 1 -3% 
inflation target. Faster increases in 
energy and food prices likely pushed 
overall inflation higher, despite an 
easing in electricity tariffs starting 
September. We expect the Bank of 
Thailand (BOT), w hich views the rise in 
inflation as largely supply -driven, to 
keep its policy rate stable at 1.00% for 
the remainder of 2026, aiming to support 
an economy with low and uneven growth.

### Page 4

## Document 367: US SECTORS CALL_ Communication Services, Materials & Energy

Source PDF: all report pdf/US SECTORS CALL_ Communication Services, Materials & Energy.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US SECTORS CALL: Communication Services, Materials & Energy
Date:    7 hours ago
US SECTORS CALL: Communication Services, Materials & Energy

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US SECTORS CALL:
Communication Services,
Materials & Energy
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 25 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Noah Klassen / Unsplash

### Page 2

Investors spent the week worrying about an AI bubble. The S&P 500
slipped by 0.5%, led by a 6.2% drop in Communication Services (MW),
which was the worst-performing S&P 500 sector (table). Yet, Information
Technology fell just 0.3%. Energy (OW) led the winning sectors with a
1.6% gain, followed by Health Care (OW), up 1.5%.
Bubbles inflate valuation multiples. This year, multiples have contracted.
The S&P 500's forward P/E has fallen to 18.6 from 22.2 at the end of 2025

(chart). Information Technology's forward P/E is down to 19.0 from 26.5.
Stocks have gotten cheaper this year as earnings growth outpaced stock
price gains.
Here's more on the Communication Services, Materials, and Energy
sectors:

### Page 3

(1) Communication Services. Meta's Q3 report last Wednesday hit the
sector hard. Its free cash flow turned negative for the first time since its
2012 IPO, and it raised its 2026 capex guidance floor to $135 billion. Its
shares fell 7.0% and Interactive Media & Services fell 4.3% on Thursday,
closing down 7.0% for the week. Alphabet and Meta dominate the sector.
Together they account for 81.8% of its market cap and 70.0% of its
forward earnings (chart).

The sector's forward earnings is up 23.9% ytd, while its stock price index
is down 2.3% (chart). Its forward P/E has dropped to 17.2 from 22.1 at the
start of the year. That puts it below the S&P 500's 18.6.
Analysts expect earnings at Interactive Media & Services, the sector's
largest industry, to rise 72.3% this year, then fall 17.6% in 2027 (chart).
Yet they expect its revenues to grow 25.0% and 22.8% over the same two

### Page 4

years. Unrealized gains on Alphabet's stakes in private AI companies,
including Anthropic, inflated this year's earnings.
The de-rating isn't limited to AI. Netflix, which accounts for more than half
of Movies & Entertainment's market cap, reported Q3 results on Tuesday.
It cut its ad revenue outlook and guided Q4 earnings below expectations.
Its stock is now down 51% from its mid-2025 record high.
(2) Materials. Materials slipped 0.1% this week. Gold and Copper were

the sector's worst-performing industries, down 8.2% and 6.3% (chart).
Copper stocks fell along with the AI trade on Thursday.
Copper futures also fell slightly last week, ending at $6.48 per pound. The
AI selloff hit industrial metals on Thursday, but copper rebounded on
Friday. Rising inventories also weighed on the red metal last week.

### Page 5

Copper flowed back into London Metal Exchange warehouses, where
inventories rose to their highest since July. Traders spent the year
shipping copper to the US ahead of potential tariffs. The US price
premium that drove those flows has turned into a discount. Copper
remains near the top of an uptrend channel that began in 2016 (chart).
Forecasts have copper earnings rising 76.3% this year and 41.2% in
2027. The forward profit margin is up to 17.1%, the highest since 2022,

yet the forward P/E has dropped to 16.4. The selloff hit stock prices, not
earnings estimates.
Analysts expect Materials earnings to rise 40.2% this year and 11.8% in
2027. Materials accounts for just 1.9% of S&P 500 forward earnings and
only 1.6% of its market cap, the smallest share in over 30 years (chart).

### Page 6

We remain overweight. Materials offers rising earnings at a shrinking
multiple.
(3) Energy. Energy is up 55.1% ytd, the best-performing sector. Its
forward earnings has risen even faster, up 76.5% ytd to a record high
(chart). Energy still has the lowest forward P/E of the 11 sectors, at 13.8,
down from 15.7 at the start of the year.
Analysts see 2026 as the peak. They expect sector earnings to rise
101.9% this year, then fall 8.0% in 2027. Refining & Marketing shows this

most clearly. Its forward profit margin has soared to a record 9.0% (chart).
Its forward P/E is 9.0, with earnings forecast to fall 11.0% in 2027.

### Page 7

Oil & Gas Equipment & Services is the exception. Analysts expect its
earnings to fall 9.7% this year, then rise 25.2% in 2027 and 18.9% in 2028
(chart).
Consensus estimates assume the oil premium fades, and we expect it will
eventually. Renewed strikes on Iran after the midterms, which the US and
Israel reportedly have planned, would challenge the fading-premium
assumption. At only 3.7% of the S&P 500's market cap and a forward P/E
of 13.8, Energy is a cheap hedge against more geopolitical turmoil.

### Page 8

clause.

資料來源：臺灣指數公司，國泰證期研究部整理

王筠婷 / 特用化學、半導體週邊 
+886-2-2326-9899#7961 
vanessawang@cathayfut.com.tw

弘塑 3131 TT  ( 電子上游–設備 ) 
 ) 
訂單能見度無虞 
投資與評價 
1. 弘塑 2Q26 毛利率僅達 38.9%， 低於我們原先及市場預期的40%水準，
主要受到添鴻產品結構轉差及新廠折舊攤提影響。 
2. 3Q26 受惠於 CoWoS 等機台認列數成長及強勁拉貨需求，營收達
22.21 億元；研究部預估 EPS 為 15.86 元。 
3. FY26 全年出機量預估維持在 220 至 240 台，展望至 FY27/FY28，
FY27 設備出貨量預估將年增 50%，FY28 亦有機會再成長 50%。 
4. 研究部預估 FY26/27 EPS 分別為 60.66 元/96.33 元，維持弘塑買進投
資建議，目標價 2,500 元，以 26x FY27 獲利預估。 
重點更新 
至 FY28 出貨量能將持續成長 
研究部認為弘塑設備出貨展望能見度將能延伸至 FY28。 由於晶圓廠 、OSAT、
記憶體客戶皆對先進封裝設備需求相當迫切，設備交期已長達 10 至 12 個
月，惟受限於短期人力供應與廠房籌備進度， FY26 全年出機量預估維持在 
220 至 240 台。展望至 FY27/FY28，FY27 設備出貨量預估將年增 50%，
FY28 亦有機會再成長 50%。 
添鴻化學品業務將於 1Q27 逐漸看見轉機 
隨 OSAT 客戶於今年 Q2/Q3 進入裝機密集期，新產線仍須經測試與量產爬
坡，相關的化學品需求通常會在設備交機後約 3~4 個月逐步提升，因此短期
營收仍受新廠折舊與稼動率偏低影響，預期添鴻的整體稼動率與毛利率有望
在 1Q27 逐漸擺脫谷底，迎來較為明顯的營運轉機。 
訂單能見度無虞 
展望 2026 年至 2028 年 ， 在各先進封裝平台強勁需求下 ， 設備產能已告全
滿，交期長達 10 至 12 個月，訂單能見度直達 2H27。就產品組合來看，
設備業務高階產品比重將逐漸提升，預估至 FY27 SoIC /CPO 等營收占比將
提升至 10~15%，添鴻化學品也將受惠於 OSAT 客戶逐漸提升拉貨動能，整
體營收與獲利成長動能明確。研究部預估 FY26/27 EPS 分別為 60.66 元
/96.33 元(YoY＋33.5%/+58.8%)。弘塑目前交易於 35/22 倍之 2026/2027 
年獲利預估。研究部維持買進投資建議，目標價 2,500 元，以 26x FY27 獲
利預估。

### Page 2

訪談內容 
3Q26 受惠於機台認列數成長，營收呈現季增 
弘塑 2Q26 營收 17.27 億元 (QoQ +8.2% / YoY +5.9%)，符合我們原先預估的 16.88 億元；毛
利率僅達 38.9% (QoQ +5.1ppts / YoY -1.7ppts)，低於我們原先及市場預期的 40%水準，主要
受到添鴻產品結構轉差及新廠折舊攤提影響 ；EPS 為 11.18 元 (QoQ -30.6% / YoY +58.6%)，
略低於我們原先預期的 11.73 元。 
3Q26 受惠於 CoWoS 等機台認列數成長及強勁拉貨需求，營收呈現季增，營收達 22.21 億元
(QoQ +28.7%/YoY+48.7%) ；研究部預估毛利率 略為季增至 40.1% (QoQ + 1.2ppts / YoY 
-0.5ppts)，產品組合持續改善，預估 EPS 為 15.86 元(QoQ +41.9%/ YoY +52.5%)。 
展望 4Q26， 受惠於先進封裝及記憶體等強勁需求的持續出機認列，研究部預期營收將持續季增，
預估 4Q26 營收將達 24.87 億元(QoQ +12.0%/YoY+15.7%)，毛利率將持穩於 40.0% (QoQ 
-0.1ppts / YoY -2.1ppts)。整體而言，預估 FY26 全年營收達 80.32 億元(YoY+23.3%)，毛利率
達 38.5%(YoY-2.6 ppts)，EPS 為 60.66 元(YoY+33.5%)。

資料來源：弘塑，國泰證期研究部整理 
至 FY28 出貨量能將持續成長 
研究部認為弘塑設備出貨展望能見度將能延伸至 FY28。由於晶圓廠、OSAT、記憶體客戶皆對先
進封裝設備需求相當迫切，設備交期已長達 10 至 12 個月，惟受限於短期人力供應與廠房籌
備進度，FY26 全年出機量預估維持在 220 至 240 台。為突破產能瓶頸，新竹租賃廠房已展開
重新配置，預計於 2026 年底投產，同時公司亦積極評估購置新竹現成廠房。 
展望至 FY27/FY28，FY27 設備出貨量預估將年增 50%，FY28 亦有機會再成長 50%。至 FY27，
OSAT 客戶的設備訂單將較 2026 年呈現倍數成長，正式接棒成為 FY27 推升公司營運成長的最
大主力。至 FY28 及中長期，受惠於先進封裝發展趨勢明確，除出貨量增加外，SoIC、CPO 及
面板級封裝等封裝技術迭代帶動設備 ASP 顯著提升（SoIC 設備較 CoWoS 設備單價增加 30% 
至 50%，面板級封裝達 2.5 至 3 倍以上），外包比例亦有望因自家產能開出而下降，整體營運呈
現量價齊揚。

### Page 3

添鴻化學品業務將於 1Q27 逐漸看見轉機 
隨 OSAT 客戶於今年 Q2/Q3 進入裝機密集期，新產線仍須經測試與量產爬坡，相關的化學品需
求通常會在設備交機後約 3~4 個月逐步提升，因此短期營收仍受新廠折舊與稼動率偏低影響，
預期添鴻的整體稼動率與毛利率有望在 1Q27 逐漸擺脫谷底，迎來較為明顯的營運轉機。 
在設備出機量持續提升 ， 且化學品業務將於FY27 看見較大轉機 ，研究部預估 FY27 營收達 115.49
億元(YoY+43.8%)。利潤率將受惠於產品組合轉好、外包比例降低而提升，預估 FY27 全年毛利
率達 43.0%(YoY+4.5ppts)，全年 EPS 為 96.33 元(YoY+58.8%)。 
訂單能見度無虞 
展望 2026 年至 2028 年，在各先進封裝平台強勁需求下 ， 設備產能已告全滿 ， 交期長達 10 至 
12 個月，訂單能見度直達 2H27。就產品組合來看，設備業務高階產品比重將逐漸提升，預估
至 FY27 SoIC/CPO 等營收占比將提升至 10~15%， 添鴻化學品也將受惠於OSAT 客戶逐漸提升
拉貨動能，整體營收與獲利成長動能明確。 
研究部預估 FY26/27 EPS 分別為 60.66 元/96.33 元(YoY＋33.5%/+58.8%)。弘塑目前交易於
35/22 倍之 2026/2027 年獲利預估 。 研究部維持買進投資建議 ， 目標價2,500 元，以 26x FY27 
獲利預估。

公司簡介 
弘塑集團由四家公司組成： 2013 年合併化學品公司添鴻、 2015 年投資日本公司 T azmo/ 
Apprecia、2018 年合併佳霖、收購太引，並投資 SVXR。
